76. **Projects and renders are given random names, an adjective and a noun joined by a hyphen, as tracker-ce's name generator makes them, and `C-r` rolls a new one on any name line** (#224). **The words:** our own lists, not tracker-ce's, whose licence restricts clones: 275 adjectives and 266 nouns in `crates/vt-model/src/names/`, one to a line, read with `include_str!`, leaning on textures, weather, places, materials, motion and music, all plain lower case letters and safe for anyone; a test checks each list has only letters and no word twice. **Names:** `vt_model::names::Names` makes `dusty-breakdown`, or one time in ten two nouns, as tracker-ce does, such as `tape-harbor`, never the same word twice. **Seeding:** from the clock, by SplitMix64, apart from the song's seed, so naming something doesn't change what `CHA` or the shape panel's random values do. **Never reused:** `taken_in` reads the target folder's names without their extensions and in lower case, as macOS folders ignore case, and a name in it is rolled again, up to 1000 times, then given the lowest free number. **Where they go:** the app makes a name when it starts, `App::project_name` until the project has a folder, and `App::suggested_name`, decision 75's hook, now takes the folder chosen: `:w` with no folder fills in that name after the folder, so `Enter` saves it, or another if the folder has it already; `:new` fills in a fresh one; `:render` fills in the project's name, its folder's or the random one before it's saved, rather than `loop`, and **numbers it** if that file is there, `dusty-breakdown-2.wav`, rather than rolling a random name, so renders stay named after their project and an earlier one isn't written over, as Ableton's export names its file after the set. The window title still says only `vimtracker` before there's a folder, so it's clear nothing is saved. **The key:** `C-r` on the command line, after tracker-ce's random key on its name keyboard. It puts a new name in place of the last part of the path on a `:w`, `:new` or `:render` line, keeping the folder, an extension, and `:render`'s flags, adds `.wav` to a render with no name yet but not for `--stems`, and does nothing on any other line. In Vim, `C-r` on the command line inserts a register, but vimtracker's registers hold steps, which can't go on a command line, so nothing it would mean is lost, and the normal-mode `C-r`, redo, is a separate table; decision 74's `Tab`, `Up`, `Down` and the list's `C-n` `C-p` don't use it. **Left out:** kit and preset saving (#157) and recording have no name lines yet; they'll start from `Names` when they do, as will the project page's Name. Added on 2026-11-08.
77. **The project page, on `Space 9`, shows and changes the song's own settings in rows laid out as the track page's, and the project's name is the song's, which a new folder takes on save-as** (#223), after the M8's Project view, Elektron's project settings and the Polyend Tracker's project screen in tracker-ce's `projectEditor`. It's `View::Project` and `Mode::Project`, `project_page.rs` and `views/project.rs`, with its own rows in the binding table, so `Space k` and the popup list them. **Where:** `Space 9`, the next free digit, as `Space 8` is now the kit picker; the audio page keeps `Space 7`, and the project page has one row for it rather than a copy. **Rows,** in two columns, how it plays on the left and how notes go in on the right: `Name` and `Folder` across the top, then `BPM` and `Scale`, `Swing` and `Root`, `Launch` and `Grid`, `Reset` and `Auto-jump`, then `Seed` and `Limiter` on the left alone, `Audio`, and the actions `Save`, `Save as`, `Render`, `Open` and `New`, each with its command dim beside it. **One path:** each row changes what its `:` command does, through it: `=` `-` `+` `_` make the `Line` the command would and run it, `K`'s lists hold `Pick::Line`, and what `i` types goes through `parse` as `bpm 137.63`, `launch 12`, `reset 0x40` or `set skip=3`, so it takes what the command takes and a mistake says what the command says. So undo is where the command has it: the tempo, swing, launch, reset, seed, limiter, grid and name are undo steps, and the scale and auto-jump aren't, as decisions 24 and 48 keep them out of undo. **Steps:** the tempo moves 1 BPM, and 10 for a large step; `l` goes onto its fine part, the digits after the point, drawn dim as the track page's fine digits are, which moves a hundredth and a tenth, carrying into the whole, and typed is the digits after the point, so `63` is `.63`; it shows two digits, or three where `:bpm` set a thousandth. Swing moves as a track's does, 1% and 5%; reset a step and a bar, down to off; the seed 1 and 10. Launch, the scale, its root and the grid are lists, one choice at a time either way as the filter type is, a launch with no name going to the named one beside it. The limiter and auto-jump are on and off: `=` on, `-` off, and `K` or `i` switch them, as the mixer's mute does. `x` puts each back as a new project has it: 120 BPM, or the fine part to `.00`, swing 50, `1bar`, no reset, seed 0, limiter on, scale off, root C, a grid of a beat and auto-jump off. **The scale on the page:** the Scale row's first choice is `off`, so it turns the scale on and off as well as choosing its kind, and the root changes only the root, unlike `:set root`, which turns the scale on, so moving through roots with it off doesn't. **Reset** is a section's, so the row is the cursor's section's, and says which once there's more than one. **Moving:** as the track page's (decision on row-preserving moves, #185): `j` and `k` with counts keep the column they left, so going down the right column past `Seed` and back comes back to it, `gg` and `G` with a count go to a row, `h` `l` `0` `$` go along it, `l` stops on the tempo's fine part, and `h` from `Scale` lands on it. `Enter`, beside `K`, does an action or opens the audio page, and `i` on anything that isn't typed does as `K` does. **Actions:** `Save` is `:w`, which with no folder yet opens the explorer and name line (decision 75); `Save as` opens them whatever, saving a copy to carry on in; `Render`, `Open` and `New` are `:render`, `:e` and `:new` with nothing after them, so `Open` and `New` refuse with unsaved changes as those do. **The name:** `Song.name`, saved in `song.ron`, `#[serde(default)]` so a song saved before it has none and takes its folder's when it's opened, which is no conversion, as nothing old is rewritten until it's saved. It's the song's own rather than the folder's, as the M8 keeps a project's name apart from its file and Elektron's project names are their own. A new project is given decision 76's random name in the song, so `App::unsaved_name` is gone and `App::project_name` is the song's name. Saving to a new folder, `:w path` or the explorer's name line, and `:new path`, name the project after the folder, which those lines start with, so the two only differ if another name is typed there, as Ableton names a set after its file on Save As. Renaming on the page doesn't move the folder, as moving folders on disk under a running project would be risky: `:w` still saves where it is, and `Save as` starts with the new name. The line saving somewhere new starts with the project's name, and **numbers it** if the folder has it, `dusty-breakdown-2`, as a render is numbered, rather than rolling another random name as decision 76 did, since the name may now be one chosen on purpose; `:new` still starts with a fresh random name. A name is up to 32 characters, with no `/` or control characters and not starting with a dot or a space or ending with a space, as it has to make a folder; `i` on Name starts with it as it is, `C-u` clears it, and `C-r` rolls a new one, not one in the folder beside this one. **The window title** is `vimtracker: name` from the start, which replaces decision 76's plain `vimtracker` before there's a folder; the Folder row says `Not saved yet`, or the folder, a long one keeping its end after an ellipsis, with `saved` or `unsaved changes`. The explorer's project preview shows the song's name where it has one. **Looks:** spartan, as the audio page: a bold `Project`, names dim, values plain, the cursor's in the cursor's colour, a dim root while the scale is off, blank lines between the name, the settings, the audio and the actions, and nothing drawn; the right column starts 40 in, within the usual 120. Added on 2026-11-08.
78. **A track's `Gate` is how much of a note's last pulse it lasts, from 1% to 100%, and `Acid`'s starts at 50%** (part 1 of #227, the time model's gate length). **The setting:** `Gate` is one of the track's own settings in the registry, `gate` in files and `LEN` on a step, listed under Machine with Pitch, so an LFO or envelope can move it and a step locks it for its row's notes, as Elektron's `LEN` sets a trig's length. On `00` to `FF`, `FF` is 100% and `80` is 50%, and `00` still gives 1%, so a note always sounds. It's in the Sound page's right-hand column under `Choke`, shown as a percentage: `=` and `-` move it a percent, `+` and `_` ten, and `i` takes a percent. Dimmed, it's the machine's own, which `x` goes back to. **What it measures:** from the note's start, as Elektron and the Metropolix measure a length, to `Gate` of the way through the note's last pulse. A `SGL`'s last pulse is its row's last, so `SGL3` at 50% lasts two and a half steps. A `MLT`'s notes last a pulse each, so each lasts `Gate` of its pulse, the Metropolix's staccato multiple. `HLD` ignores it and ties into the next row's note, which closes its own gate, and `RST` is unchanged. A delayed note's length counts from its delayed start. #227 left open whether a `SGL` takes `Gate` of its first pulse or of its whole row. The first pulse was rejected because 100% has to hold a note as it did before: a `SGL4` would jump from under one pulse at 99% to four at 100%, and on `Acid` a long note that doesn't slide would need an `OFF`. Scaling the whole row was rejected because it halves a long note, where a gap before the next note is what a shorter gate means. The last pulse is how a 303 plays a tied note, holding its gate through the tied steps and closing it halfway through the last, so `SGL2` on `Acid` is a note and a tie. **How a note ends:** the gate closing lets the note go as `OFF` does, starting the release of its envelopes, so the release shapes the gap; it isn't a cut. It also ends a `RET`'s repeats, which play only while the gate's open, as a ratchet on a short note stays short; an `OFF` or `KIL` before it still ends the note sooner. At 100% nothing is scheduled, so a song with no `Gate` set plays as before, sample for sample, and the golden renders are unchanged. The length is worked out on the step's time without swing, so a swung step's gate isn't stretched or squeezed with it; the Groove (#227's part 2) can revisit this. **`Acid`:** its `Gate` starts at `80`, as a 303's gate closes about halfway through each step unless it slides (Whittle, Open303's `setNoteLength`), and its own release, from decision 46, takes it 40 dB down 11 ms after the gate closes and 60 dB down in 18 ms, measured in `tests/gate_length.rs`. A slide is `HLD` on the row that slides away, as a 303's slide is set on the step it leaves (#220's second item), so it holds the gate into the note it slides to, which then closes its own halfway. So that its filter and accent sweep still carry from note to note across the gaps, as on a 303, a released `Acid` voice keeps running silently until its sweep has drained, rather than stopping when its envelope's release ends; with the gaps, a run of accents builds up less than when every note holds, as the sweep charges only while the gate's open. Every other machine starts at 100%, so pads and held notes are unchanged. This is the general setting that replaces #220's `Acid`-only one. Speed and the Groove, the rest of #227, come later. Added on 2026-11-08. Decision 79 gives a row a length of its own in its gate cell, which `LEN` no longer sets, and makes what moves `Gate` move each note's length up or down from its own.
79. **A row's gate cell holds how long its note lasts, and what moves `Gate` moves that length up or down rather than replacing it** (part of #227, after decision 78). The author decided that a note's length belongs to the note's part of the row, and that FX columns and lanes modulate it. **The cell:** a third part after the gate and its pulses, as in `SGL1 80`, on the `00` to `FF` scale `Gate` uses, drawn dim, and `--` with none, which plays at the track's `Gate`. The whole cell is 7 characters, so the first FX column starts 3 further right; at 120 columns the phrase view still has its overview of the other tracks beside it. In step text the length is a field after the gate's, `C-4 64 SGL1 80 cut:40`, as two hex digits can't be taken for an FX slot, and a row without one is written as before. It's a cursor stop of its own in the gate's column group, as an FX value's fine digits are in their slot's: `l` goes onto it, two hex digits in Insert or after `r` set it, `=` and `-` move it by one and `+` and `_` by 16, `x` and `Backspace` take it away, and `K` there chooses the gate and keeps it. Being in the gate's group, it's copied, pasted and merged with the cell, and `x` on the gate's own part empties the cell, length and all. A length typed into an empty cell makes it one pulse of `SGL`, which is how it played. `r` across a selection fills it on the rows with a note, as velocity is filled, and `.` puts it on another row's cell, keeping that row's gate. An empty length moved with `=` or `-` starts from the track's `Gate`, as that's what it plays at, and only on a row with a note, so a selection doesn't put cells on empty rows. The shape panel doesn't fill it yet. **The rule:** a row's own length is the base. Without one, the base is the track's `Gate` as it is when the note starts, a `LEN` lock or a lane's value included, so `LEN` only moves rows without a length. Modulation adds to the base, as an LFO's offset adds to every other setting's held value (`offsets` in `track_state.rs`): an LFO or envelope on `Gate`, and `LDLT`, both on rows with a length of their own and without. The sum is kept from 1% to 100%, and at 100% nothing is scheduled, as before. `HLD` ignores all of it; a lock on a `HLD` row carries into the note it ties into, which closes its own gate with it, as a tie is still the same note. **`LDLT`** is a step setting of the track's, `gatedelta` in files, named "Gate up or down" and listed under Machine beside `LEN`, with "Gate length, up or down from the note's own" as its meaning. It's centred on `80`, which leaves a note alone, and one step of it is one step of `Gate`, so `00` takes half a pulse off and `FF` adds half, shown from `-50 %` to `+50 %`. It sweeps, so it has fine digits and glides. It's a registry setting of the track's whose held value is used as an offset, rather than a lockable modulation value like `DEP1`. `DEP1` belongs to an LFO, sets how far it swings and has its base on the Mod page; a change to a note's length has no modulator to belong to. As one of the track's settings it gets locks, holds, glides, lanes, `K` and the step text from the registry with nothing new, and as its base is always `80` it needs nothing on the track or the track page. It's left out of the LFOs' and envelopes' `Dest`, as moving it would only move `Gate`, which they can already. The label was free, as was `GOFS`, the other considered; `LDLT` sits with `LEN`. **Live recording** (decision 55) writes a held note's length in its gate cell rather than in an `OFF`, and leaves the row's pulses as they are, as recording mustn't reshape a loop or change its length. The length is what was held of the row's last pulse, at most `FE`, so the note ends where it was let go. A note held past the row's end is `FF`, which rings on until the next note or something else ends it, as a held note does. A note let go before its row's last pulse, as a tap on a row of several pulses is, gets `00`, the least the cell can say, so it lasts just into that pulse. While the key is down the cell is `FF`, and a note still held when its row comes round again ends there. The search for an FX column to put an `OFF` in is gone. **Alternatives:** a length only as `LEN`, decision 78's, is what this changes, as a lane of `LEN` replaced every note's own length rather than moving it. A first version made a recorded row last as many pulses as its hold, rounded to the nearest step, so a hold was exact to the cell's resolution. The author rejected it on review, as it moved every later row and changed the track's loop length, so the track drifted against the others. A note held past its row now rings on to the next one rather than stopping where it was let go, which is the cost. Added on 2026-11-08.
80. **A Groove is a track's feel: a pattern of 1 to 16 pushes in ticks, how late or early each step lands, with a velocity for each step and a seeded humanise, chosen for the project, for a track, or on a step with `GRV`, and swing is now grooves** (part 2 of #227, the time model's feel), after the M8's Groove view and its "Common Groove Examples" (manual pp. 26 and 81), Ableton's groove pool and the MPC 60's swing. **The pattern:** `Groove`, in `vt-model`'s `groove.rs`, has 1 to 16 steps, counted from the start of the running count. Each has a push in ticks, 240 to a step, from -120 to 120, half a step, as far as swing at 75% went, and a velocity in percent, 100 unless changed, up to 200. Its humanise moves each step at random up to 24 ticks either way, a tenth of a step, from a generator of the track's own seeded from the project's seed as `CHA`'s is, but on another stream, so a render repeats and `CHA` rolls as it did. **Pushes sum to zero, as the first step stays on the grid.** #227 agreed that pushes sum to zero, so a groove never changes the tempo or how long a loop takes, which is the M8's rule that a groove's tick counts add up to the pattern's. Written as the M8 writes them, as how much longer each step is, today's swing at 66% is `+77 -77`, which sums to zero but reads as the first step being late when it's the second. Written as where each step lands, which is #227's push, it's `0 +77`, which doesn't sum to zero, and pushes centred to sum to zero, `-38 +39`, would move the downbeat and change every swung render. The two ways of writing it are the same thing: lengths that add up to the pattern's are landings whose first is 0. So a push is where its step lands, as Ableton's grooves, the MPC's swing and Elektron's micro-timing show it, and the first step's push is always 0, as it's the one the others are pushed from. The rule then can't be broken, so nothing is rebalanced and nothing refused on saving, and changing a push moves that one step, where on the M8 changing one moves the next with it. A push can be either way, so a step can land early. **Presets:** Straight; Swing 52, 54, 56, 58, 60, 62, 64, 66 and 70, each two steps with the second as late as the old `swing_ticks` put it, so they play as `:swing` did at those percentages, frame for frame, and the golden render with swing at 66.7%, now a groove of the project's own, is unchanged; MPC 54, 58, 62, 66 and 71, a bar of 16 steps of the MPC 60's sixteenth swing at its own settings, each landing on the MPC's clock of 96 to a quarter, 10 of our ticks, so MPC 66 is the triplet, 80 ticks, where Swing 66 is 77; Late last, the M8's "Swing Last Step", a bar on the grid but its last step, 40 ticks late, as the M8's `07 05` at its 24 to a quarter; and two bars of push and pull, Push, with the off-beat eighths 12 ticks early and the sixteenths between them 6, and Pull, with the backbeats 20 ticks late and the sixteenths between 8. Every preset's velocities are 100%, as one that quietened some steps would quieten a kick on them too. A groove is kept in songs by its name, as settings are by key, so adding a preset doesn't move the others. **Where it's chosen:** `Song.groove`, the project's, Straight in a new project, and `Track.groove`, a track's own or none to follow the project's, in place of `swing` on both. `:groove name` and `:tgroove name` set them, `:tgroove off` follows the project's again, and either alone opens a list; a name is written as `swing-66`, `mpc-58` or `custom-1`, with case, spaces and hyphens ignored, and Tab completes them. `:swing 66` is kept as a shorthand for the Swing groove at that percent, or Straight at 50, and `:swing 66.7` is refused, as there's no groove at it; `:tswing` is gone. On the track page `Swing` is now `Groove`: `=` and `-` move through the list one at a time from what it plays, `K` and `i` list them with the project's first, it's dimmed while it follows the project's, and `x` follows it again. The status line says `Groove Swing 66`, with `(track)` after a track's own. No song is converted, so one saved with `swing` plays straight. **`GRV` on a step:** `groove`, labelled `GRV`, is a registry setting of the track's, a list whose first choice is "Project", the project's groove, followed by every groove in order, and whose held value starts as the track's own, or `00` with none. So locks, holds, lanes, `K`, the picker and the step text come from the registry with nothing new. It's listed under Machine with `LEN`, and left out of the LFOs' and envelopes' `Dest`, as a groove is chosen rather than swept. Unlike any other setting it's read before its step plays, as it says when the step lands: a `GRV` on the step's row, or in a lane at the step, gives the step its groove; if not, and the row's first pulse has a note, which will end any lock, the held value does; otherwise whatever the track holds. A lock lasts its note, as Elektron's do, so a fill goes straight with `GRV 01` on each of its rows, or with `GRV 01h` at its start and a hold of the track's groove after, as the M8's `GRV` holds until the next. The engine only looks for it on rows when the song has a `GRV` somewhere, so other songs don't pay for the walk to the row twice. **What it counts against:** the running count every track shares, the one swing counted, from the song's start or the last reset, a section that resets or `:reset`, not each track's own loop or each phrase's rows. So tracks of different lengths swing together, and the groove stays on the beat; a 7-row phrase drifts against a 2-step swing, as a phrase of that length drifts against a real swung beat. A section that doesn't reset carries the count on, as swing did, so a groove counts from the start of the section the count began in rather than each section's own. Counting by a phrase's rows, the M8's way, could be a per-track option later. **How it meets the rest:** each pulse of a row is a step, so a `MLT`'s pulses each get their own push and velocity. Lanes are read when their step lands, so they're grooved, as swing grooved them. `DEL`, `KIL`, `OFF` and `RET` count from where the step lands. A step's velocity scales the note's own, rounded and kept at `7F` at most, so it can make an accent or lose one. A note's gate length, decision 78's, which used the step's time without swing, now follows the grooved step: it runs from the note's start to where its last pulse lands, then its `Gate` share of how long the groove makes that pulse, from where it lands to where the next does, so the long first step of a swung pair holds its note longer and the short second shorter. With Straight that's the old sum, so unswung renders are unchanged. Humanise moves a note's start and end together. Live recording, decision 55, puts a note on the row whose grooved start is nearer, writes it once that row has landed so it isn't played twice, and writes its length as a share of its last pulse as the groove makes it, with the track's own groove or the project's; a step's `GRV` isn't looked at. #146's nudge will add on top: grid, then groove, then nudge. **Early steps:** a step pushed early has to be played before its place on the grid, so the engine works every step out ahead of its place by as far as any groove the song can play lands one early: the project's, the tracks' own and any a `GRV` in the song chooses, found in `Published::new` as `lead`. With none early it's 0, and steps are worked out on the grid as they always were, so nothing else moves. With a lead, a section change, a cue and a launch that stops a track happen that much early, up to half a step; the playhead stays on the step heard until the next one's place on the grid; a render works its pass's first step out again from the top; and a step can land no later than half a step late, which is no later than the next is worked out, so steps always play in order. #146's early nudge can use the same. **On screen:** nothing changes in the grid. Steps are drawn on their rows, and the playhead moves on the grid. **The project page** keeps the project's own grooves, eight of them, `Custom 1` to `Custom 8`, in `Song.grooves`, each a bar on the grid until it's made. Its `Swing` row is now `Groove`, a list as `Launch` is. Under the settings, before `Audio`, a groove is drawn and changed: `Edit`, which groove, the project's until another is chosen, with `K` listing them all; `Steps`, with `Humanise` beside it; then the pushes, a tick above a faint line for a step that lands late and below it for one that lands early, one character high in eighths, as tall as its push is against half a step, in the text colour, with each push under it, then each velocity, and their units dim after. `=` and `-` move a push a tick and `+` and `_` ten, a velocity 1% and 10%, the steps 1 and 4, and the humanise 1 and 4; `i` types a number, and `x` puts it back on the grid, at 100%, at 16 steps or with no humanise. Changing a groove that comes with the program copies it into the first of the project's own not yet made, which the page then shows and the project plays if it played the one copied, all as one undo step, as an Elektron preset is copied to be changed. The project page was chosen over a page of its own, as grooves are the project's and there are only a handful, and the page already has rows and the keys to change them; the M8's Groove view is a page of its own, and grooves could move to one if they grow. **Alternatives:** pushes as how much longer each step is, the M8's way, which sum to zero as written but don't read as where a step lands, and where changing one moves the next; landings centred to sum to zero, which move the downbeat and can't make the old swing exactly; rebalancing the other steps when one changes, which isn't needed; only late pushes, to avoid working ahead, which push and pull feels and #146 both need; and a lead of half a step for every song, rejected so that songs without an early groove play exactly as before. Added on 2026-11-08. Decision 81 adds #146's nudge on top, and lets a nudged row land after the next step is worked out. Decision 82 has each track count its own steps, at its own speed, from the same origin.
81. **A row's nudge moves its note part up to just under half a step early or late, in 24ths of a step, on top of where the groove lands its step, while the FX columns with lanes of their own stay on the grid** (#146, M18 "Time and feel"), as agreed with the author on the issue, after Elektron's micro-timing, which moves a trig and its locks both ways by just under half a step. **The field:** `Step.nudge`, an `i8` in 24ths of a step, from -11 to 11, 0 on the grid. A 24th is `NUDGE_TICKS`, 10 of a step's 240 ticks, finer than `DEL`'s sixths and about Elektron's resolution; 11 is the most below half a step, Elektron's limit, which keeps a row's note from passing the next row's on the grid. It's drawn between the velocity and the gate cell as a sign and one hex digit, `-3` or `+B`, dim, and blank on a row on the grid, so only a nudged row catches the eye and the columns never move; the first FX column starts 3 further right, at 18. Hex was chosen over decimal, which would need 3 characters for `+11`, as the grid is hex everywhere else; the status line shows it as `3/24 of a step early`. **Keys:** it's a cursor stop of its own after the velocity, in the velocity's column group, as the two are how the note is played, the way the gate's length is in the gate's group (decision 79). So a selection of velocities copies, pastes, merges and clears their nudges too, and `x` on either part clears only that one, as velocity and nudge mean separate things, where `x` on the gate's own part empties its cell. `=` and `-` move it by a 24th and `+` and `_` by four, a sixth of a step, `DEL`'s unit, and it stays within 11. As velocity and length are, a nudge is only given to a row with a note, so `=` on a selection leaves empty rows on the grid, while one a row already has moves. In Insert, or after `r`, it's one digit from `0` to `B`, late unless `-` comes first, with `+` or `=` first saying late again, so `-3` is three early and `0` puts it back; a digit past `B` is refused with a message, and auto-jump moves on once the digit's in. `Backspace` clears it. `r` across a selection fills it on rows with a note, and `.` repeats it. `K` there says it chooses on the gate and FX columns, and shapes refuse it. **Step text:** a field after the velocity, `C-4 64 -3 SGL1 80 cut:40`, as a sign and one digit is neither a gate nor an FX slot; a row on the grid is written as before, and `+0` and lowercase digits are refused. **What moves with it,** as #146 agreed: the note, its velocity and gate, the row's own FX columns, those without a lane, `DEL`, `KIL`, `OFF`, `RET` and `CHA` from any column, as they're about the note, counting from where it now starts, and the envelopes, which start with it. What stays is what keeps time apart from the note: a column with a lane, whose values land where the groove lands the step, the LFOs, and the groove itself. The order is grid, then groove, then nudge. A note nudged early on a step where a lane changes a value starts with the value before, and the lane's value comes in on the grid; no "catch" rule. **The note's length:** its end moves with its start, as Elektron's micro-timing moves the whole trig, so a nudge keeps its length, which decision 80's gate length already gives when it's measured from the note's start. **`MLT`:** the nudge belongs to the row, so every pulse of a `MLT` moves by it together, with each pulse's own `DEL`, `KIL`, `OFF` and `RET`. **Locks:** the last note's locks end where the step's first part lands. On a step whose note is nudged late, that's its lanes, so a lane's lock there lasts the nudged note rather than being ended by it, and the note before loses its locks that much earlier; on one nudged early it's the note, as before. `CHA` is rolled once, by the step's first part to land, and the other part is told the result. **Scheduling:** the engine already worked steps out ahead by the earliest groove the song can play (decision 80's `Published::lead`). With a nudge, `lead` adds the earliest nudge in the song, so with only late nudges, or none, it's what it was, and songs without nudges are worked out and land exactly as before: their golden renders are unchanged. `Published` keeps the groove's own lead apart as `groove_lead`, which a step's push is still kept within, and says whether any row has a nudge, `nudges`; only then does the engine look up each step's row for its nudge when working it out, as `GRV` is looked for only when the song has one. A nudged step is worked out into two parts, `Part::Lanes` where the groove lands it and `Part::Row` where the nudge does, and one without, `Part::Whole`, as every step was. A groove can push a step 120 ticks and a nudge 110 more, so with both a row's note can land up to 230 ticks late, after the next step is worked out, which breaks decision 80's rule that a step lands before the next is worked out. Clamping groove and nudge together to half a step was considered and rejected, as it takes most of the nudge away on a swung step. Instead the scheduler handles later landings: each track keeps up to four parts waiting, two steps' lanes and rows, as a part never lands later than 230 ticks after its step's place, and the step after next is worked out no sooner than 250 ticks after it; the parts are kept with the chain and place they were worked out at, and the count their lanes read, so one landing after the next step's work, a cue or a section change still plays its own row; they play in the order they land, a step's first part before its other when they land together; and with no room, which only a big change of tempo could bring about, the earliest plays at once. In a song with no nudges, every part waiting is played when the next step is worked out, as before. **Order:** a track's notes always play in order. Without a groove, a nudge can't take a row's note past the next row's, as both together are under a step. With a groove's pushes too it can: then the later row's note part is held back to land with the earlier one's, rather than before it, and is the note left sounding, as decision 80's latest late step and earliest early step meet; lanes are never held back. Letting the later note play first and the earlier one end it was rejected, as it plays the rows out of order. **Live recording** (decisions 55 and 79) writes how far from where its row lands, as the track's groove lands it, the note was played as the row's nudge, to the nearest 24th and at most 11, where it was dropped before, so a note goes back where it was played. It's written once its row has landed, as before, so it isn't played twice in that pass. **Alternatives:** Renoise's delay column and the M8's `DEL`, which move only the note, and only late; a nudge that moves the lanes on its step too, which would make a lane act early or late only where notes happen to be nudged; a lead of half a step for every song; and a field in decimal 24ths, which takes a character more. Added on 2026-11-09.
82. **A track's `Speed` is how long each of its steps is, as a note division from `1/32` to `1/4` with the triplets between, set on the track page or on a step with `RATE`, and everything on the track counts its own steps while the song counts sixteenths** (part 3 of #227, the time model's rate), after the Metropolix's `CLOCK DIV`, Elektron's per-track scale and the M8's triplet and double-speed grooves, which #227 moved into a setting of their own. **The setting:** `Speed`, in `vt-model`'s `speed.rs`, is `1/32`, `1/16T`, `1/16`, `1/8T`, `1/8`, `1/4T` or `1/4`, 120 to 960 ticks a step, and `1/16` unless changed. Elektron's ×3/2 and ×3/4 are the sixteenth and eighth triplets, so #227's "perhaps ×3/4 and ×3/2" are in the list already. `Track.speed` keeps it, left out of a file at `1/16`. It's a registry setting of the track's, `speed` in files and `RATE` on a step, a list listed under Machine with `LEN` and `GRV`, so locks, holds, lanes, `K`, the picker and the step text come from the registry with nothing new, and `RATE 04` is an eighth. The label is `RATE`, as the author dislikes Elektron's `SPD` and `MULT` for rates, and values are shown as note divisions. It's left out of the LFOs' and envelopes' `Dest`, as a speed is chosen rather than swept. On the track page it's the Sound page's right-hand column under `Gate`: `=` and `-` move through the list one at a time and stop at either end, `K` and `i` list them with what each plays against the song's sixteenths, `x` goes back to `1/16`, and it's dimmed at `1/16`. **What follows a track's step:** each pulse of a gate is one of the track's steps, so `MLT` and `HLD` follow, and so does a lane, which counts steps. Everything that's a share of a step is a share of the track's: `DEL`, `KIL`, `OFF` and `RET`'s sixths, a nudge's 24ths, a groove's pushes and humanise, a glide's rows and a note's `Gate`, so they're always the same fraction of the track's own step. The frames for a share are worked out exactly from the tempo's origin, so at `1/16` they're what they were, frame for frame. The LFOs stay on the song's time, as their rates are note divisions of the song's, and envelopes are in milliseconds, so they start with each note however long its step is. **The song counts sixteenths:** sections, `:reset`, `Launch::Every`, the song view and renders count the song's steps, as Elektron's master length does, so a section's length is its longest chain at that chain's speed, 16 rows at `1/8` being 32 sixteenths, and a triplet chain that doesn't end on a sixteenth is rounded up; four `1/16T` rows are 3. A loop's render is that section's length, or its reset, as before, so a lone track at `1/8` renders its whole chain, and a track whose cycle doesn't divide it won't join up, as a polymeter doesn't now. **Where a track's steps fall:** each track has a clock of its own in the engine, `Clock` in `track_state.rs`: where its next step falls on the grid, its running count there, and an anchor, where it last lined up. A track lines up, its next step falling on the song's step there and its steps counted from it, wherever its chain starts: at the song's start, a reset or `:reset`, a section that gives it a new chain, the song wrapping round, and a launch. Where that comes partway through a longer step, as at the end of a 7-step section for a track at `1/8`, the step is cut short, as an Elektron pattern change restarts every track. A track that carries on into a section with the same chain keeps its clock, so a triplet track in a section of 16 sixteenths, 24 of its steps, runs on its own grid and stays there; one in a section that its steps don't divide drifts against it until its chain next starts. Playing from a step works each track's clock out at its own speed from the start or the last reset, as playing up to it would, and a track partway through a longer step there comes in on its next; playing from a row of the cursor's track starts the song at the sixteenth that row falls in, with that track starting its row there. **A change of speed:** the speed a step plays at is worked out before it plays, as `GRV`'s groove is: a `RATE` on its row or in a lane at it, or else what the track holds, which a note on the row's first pulse ends any lock of. A lock lasts its note, so the empty rows after a locked note go at its speed too, and held with `h` it stays. A step at a new speed starts where it falls, and lasts until the next of that speed's steps counted from the anchor, so the track keeps to the grid of whatever speed it's at: a `1/8` on the second sixteenth lasts one sixteenth, then the track plays on the eighths, and a `1/16` on a triplet's second step lasts to the next sixteenth. A change on the track page is heard from the next step in the same way. Taking each lock's speed from the step it starts on, which would leave the track off its new grid for good, was rejected, as was waiting for the bar. **The groove counts each track's own steps** (revisiting decision 80): each track counts its own steps from the same origin, the song's start or the last reset, rather than one running count all tracks share, so a track at `1/8` swings its eighths, as #227 agreed, and its pushes are shares of its step, twice as long in time. A track that lines up as its chain starts carries its count on, as the shared count did across a section that doesn't reset. At `1/16` each track's count is the shared count, so every song without a speed swings as before, and both golden renders are unchanged sample for sample. **Live recording** (decisions 55, 79 and 80) goes by the cursor's track's own steps: the engine reports where each track is heard, its count, how far through its step and how long that step is, and a note goes on the row whose grooved start is nearer, as long as the track's speed makes its steps, with its nudge in 24ths of that step and its length a share of its pulse. **Each track's playhead** is drawn where that track is, from the same report, as is each lane's. **Scheduling:** steps are worked out ahead of their place by `Published::lead` (decisions 80 and 81), which now takes the earliest groove and nudge as shares of the longest step any track can play, its own speed or a `RATE` in the song: an early push at `1/4` is four times as early in time. Every track works its steps out that far ahead, with the song's step at the same lead, so every step is worked out in the order it falls on the grid, and a section change, a cue or a reset always comes between the steps before it and the steps after, whatever their speeds. A song with no early groove or nudge has a lead of 0 at any speed, and one with every track at `1/16` has the lead it had. A track's step that falls between the song's steps is worked out on its own, before the song's next, and one that falls with the song's is worked out with it, after the song's step has moved the section on. With a long lead, a fast track has several steps worked out before the one heard has landed, so each track keeps up to 24 parts waiting, enough for a `1/32` track against a `1/4` track's earliest groove and nudge, and with any speed in the song only what's due plays when the next step is worked out, as with nudges, rather than everything waiting; with no room the earliest still plays at once. Every step still lands within half a step of its own place, a share of its own step, so a track's notes play in order. **Alternatives:** a finer master clock that every track's steps fall on, which would have changed every count the song keeps; each track working its steps out at its own lead, which could bring a section change before a step of the section before; scaling the groove only at `1/16`, which #227 left open and the author decided against; and a speed per phrase, so a fill can double up, which is left for later, as are speeds slower than a quarter, Elektron's ×1/8. Added on 2026-11-09.
83. **In Insert on the Note column, `'` accents a note and `/` slides from it: tapped after a note, each switches on or off on the row just written, and held, each goes on every note played while it's down** (part of #220, its third item), after the 303's pattern writing. **Prior art:** on a TB-303 or TB-03, the pitches go in first, and then, stepping through with TAP, ACCENT or SLIDE pressed with TAP held puts one on that step, and pressed again takes it off; on the x0xb0x a step's ACCENT and SLIDE light while it's written and NEXT moves on; Roland's TB-303 plugin has an ACCENT button that accents what its preview keyboard plays. So on a 303 they're toggled on a step, after its note. Elektron's trig conditions and locks go on the trig held while a setting is turned, which is the held form. The M8, tracker-ce and Renoise have no accent at all: the M8 and Renoise type a velocity into its own column, and tracker-ce keeps a velocity only as a volume FX, which recording from a MIDI device can fill. **Keys:** a 303 has ACCENT and SLIDE past the top of its keyboard, and these are past ours, which stops at `l` as Ableton's does: `'` is a stress mark, and `/` a slide in guitar tab. Both are in reach of the right little finger from `;`, and neither is a note, an octave or velocity key, `;`, `:` or `p`, which stays unmapped as in Ableton; `q` `r` `i` `n` `m` and `b` were the other free keys, none with a meaning to remember them by. They're the first rows of the binding table for Insert, so `Space k` lists them, and the Note column looks them up there. **After a note:** a tap goes on the row the last note went on, as long as the cursor is where that left it, so with auto-jump it's the row just left, and without, the cursor's own, which is the same row; after an arrow, or once Insert is left and entered again, it's the cursor's row. That keeps the 303's order, note then accent, though our note moves on at once. **With a note:** a note played while the key's down is written with it, as one change with the note, and the key's release then does nothing, which is how a tap is told from a hold: no note while it was down. So the tap acts on release, not press, which costs a tap's length in feedback; acting on press would have put it on the row before when the key was meant to be held. Notes rolled under a held key all get it, as each note key writes (decision 54), and both keys can be held at once. A held flag key's repeats, and kitty's presses of it, do nothing, as for a held note; leaving Insert or losing focus lets go of them without acting. **Accent:** on is `7F`, the full level, as a 303's accent is all or nothing and `Acid` takes any velocity from `70` up as one (decision 46); off is `Vel`, the velocity notes are written with, unless that's `70` or above, when it's `64`, a new song's. A row whose velocity is empty plays at full, so counts as accented. A velocity other than those two, say `40` accented and back, comes back as `Vel`, as there's nowhere to keep it. **Slide:** on makes the gate `HLD`, keeping the cell's pulses and its length, so the row lasts as long and, though `HLD` ignores the length (decision 79), it's there again when the slide comes off; off makes it `SGL`, and a cell that's then as an empty cell plays, `SGL1` with no length, is emptied, so a row slid and unslid is as it was. A `MLT` slid and unslid comes back a `SGL`. **Rows:** both need a note on the row, and say so otherwise. They work on every track, as velocity and `HLD` mean the same everywhere. Each switch is one change to undo, as a note is. **Recording:** a note played with a flag's key down is written with it, a held slide as a `HLD` with the row's pulses, but a tap has no row to go on, as a recorded note is written only once its row starts, so it says to hold the key instead. **Shown:** the message line says `Accent on`, `Accent off`, `Slide on`, `Slide off` or `Accent and slide on`; the grid shows the `7F` and the `HLD`. **Alternatives:** Shift with a note key for an accent, which would leave a slide wanting another modifier and the 303's after-the-note order out; acting on the cursor's row only, which with auto-jump is the row after the note; and a press that acts at once, which can't also be held. Added on 2026-11-09.
84. **A row's `CND` plays it only when a trig condition holds, after Elektron's: pass `x` of every `y`, `FILL`, `PRE`, `NEI` and `1ST` with their opposites, where a pass is each time the track plays the row since its chain started** (#230, the first of #229's sequencer logic). **Prior art:** Elektron's trig conditions are one list on a trig: a percent chance, `FILL` and `!FILL`, `PRE` and `!PRE` (whether the last condition evaluated on the track was true, with `PRE` and `NEI` themselves not evaluated), `NEI` and `!NEI` (the same for the track before), `1ST` and `!1ST` (the first time the pattern plays), and `A:B` from `1:2` to `8:8`, counting the pattern's loops; a failed condition skips the trig and its locks, and fill is on while a key is held, or for one cycle when it's tapped. The M8's `NTH xy` skips what's left or right of it by the phrase's or table's loop count, and its `CHA xy` gives the note and the other FX separate chances. Renoise's `-Yxx` is a chance, with `00` playing one of a line's notes. The Metropolix has a `PROB` per stage. **What's taken:** Elektron's list, as it's the one that's known to make loops evolve, without its percent, which is `CHA`. Renoise's one-of-a-line and the M8's separate gates for note and FX are left out for now. **The command:** `CND`, an event, so it's picked with `K` among the events and its value is two hex digits, as the issue proposed and as every other event's is. `12` to `88` is pass `x` of every `y`, with `y` from 2 to 8, so the grid reads `CND 12` as `1:2`; `01` to `08` are `FILL`, `!FILL`, `PRE`, `!PRE`, `NEI`, `!NEI`, `1ST` and `!1ST`, in Elektron's order, odd for the condition and even for its opposite. Choosing `CND` with `K` goes straight on to a list of the conditions in groups, with Elektron's names and each one's value and meaning, and `K` on a `CND`'s value lists them again; `=` and `-` step through the same list, and `+` and `_` move a pass to a longer or shorter cycle, `1:2` to `1:3`, so neither lands on a value that isn't a condition. A typed value that isn't one is refused with a message, and one in a file is left out, as if the slot were empty. **What counts as a pass:** each time the track plays the row since its chain started, worked out from the track's count rather than kept, so it's the count over the chain's length, times the times the row's phrase is in the chain, plus the times it came before this place. A phrase played four times in its chain counts four passes each time round, which is the M8's phrase loop and the issue's "each time the track plays the row", and with different phrases in a chain it's the chain's loops, Elektron's pattern loops. A `CND` in a lane counts the lane's loops, its count over its length. As it's the count, it follows what the count does: each track's own steps at its own speed (decision 82), so a track at `1/8` counts its own passes; it starts over wherever the chain starts, at the top, a section that brings the chain in or resets, a launch and `:reset`; a section that carries the same chain on keeps counting; and playing from a step counts the passes before it, as playing up to it would. Counting the song's sections or resets, which every track would share, was rejected, as tracks of different lengths would then count passes they hadn't played, and so was a count kept per row, which playing from a step couldn't work out. **What a failed condition skips:** the row's note, its events and its own locks, those in columns without a lane, as Elektron skips a trig with its locks, so a `CND` on a row with no note gates its locks. A column with a lane keeps to its own loop, as its values keep time apart from the note (decision 81). A step's `GRV` and `RATE` still time it, as they're chosen before it lands. Two conditions on a row must both hold. `CHA` is left as it was decided in issue 15, skipping only the note with the row's other FX still applying, so songs with `CHA` and both golden renders are unchanged; every `CHA` on a row is rolled even when a condition fails the row, so its random stream, the track's own, is the same with conditions or without, and conditions use no random numbers. **`PRE` and `NEI`:** each track keeps whether its last condition passed, which is what its last row with a `CND` or `CHA` came to, all of them together; `PRE` and `NEI` read it and, as on Elektron's, aren't counted in it, so after a `1:2`, a `PRE` row plays with it and a `!PRE` row in its place. `NEI` reads the track to the left, so the first track's never plays and its `!NEI` always does. Each is read as the row's first part lands, so on the same step, the track to the left has decided first unless its groove or nudge lands it later. Nothing has passed when playing starts, from the top or a step. **Fill:** `Space l`, beside `Space m` and `Space s`, which a tap switches and a hold keeps on until it's let go, as mute and solo are (#200); `l` is free after `Space` and is in "fill", and `Space f` is the picker's. Elektron's tap that fills for one cycle was left out, as with tracks of their own lengths there's no one cycle to end on. Fill is a live control in the engine, sent with `SetFill`, never saved and never on in a render. **Shown:** the cell shows `CND` and its value, as other events do; on the status line it's named with its meaning, `1:2, pass 1 of every 2` or `PRE, when this track's last condition passed`, and the transport says `FILL` while fill is on. While playing, the row under the playhead is faint when its `CND` or `CHA` skipped its note, from a flag on each track's place in the telemetry, so the logic can be followed. **Alternatives:** a command for each condition, which would add seven events and values that mean nothing; the condition's name drawn in the cell, which the value's fixed two digits and cursor don't allow; gating the FX apart from the note, as the M8 does, which can come later as `CND` values of its own; and changing `CHA` to skip locks too, which would have changed what every song with it plays. Added on 2026-11-09.
85. **A row's `ACU` moves its note on each time the row plays, by scale degrees with the scale on and semitones with it off, after the Metropolix's accumulator, within the track's `Up` and `Down` limits, and the `Acc` row on the Mod page says whether the row or the whole track moves, what happens at a limit, when it adds and when it starts again** (#231, the second of #229's sequencer logic). **Prior art:** the Metropolix's ACCUM and LIM (manual v1.6.5, pp. 99–101 and 125–133): each stage has a transpose amount in scale degrees and a trigger, `Stage`, `Pulse` or `Ratch`, whose table against the gate types says a `HOLD` adds once a stage play, a `MULTI` on every pulse or every ratchet, a `SINGLE` on its first pulse or that pulse's ratchets, and a `REST` never; a stage that `PROB` stops counts as a rest. LIM has positive and negative limits from 1 to 28 degrees, Mode `Stage` or `Track` (with C D E F and +1 on the first stage, `Stage` plays `C D E F | D D E F | E D E F` and `Track` plays `C D E F | D E F G | E F G A`), an Order of `Wrap`, `Pendulum`, `Random` or `Hold`, a Polarity, `Unipolar` (between the stage's pitch and the limit the amount points to) or `Bipolar` (between both limits), and a Reset of `Manual`, `Auto` (whenever the sequence resets) or `Aux/Mod`; its note names leave the accumulator out. The M8's `REP` repeats the last command, adding each step until `RTO`'s limit, and a table at `TIC00` moves on a row per note. Renoise's `-Uxx` and `-Ixx` are the same idea per tick. **The command:** `ACU`, an event, picked with `K`, as the issue proposed `ACC` but that is Accent's label (decision 29) and an event's label can't match a setting's; `ACU` reads as "accu", and events have three letters. **The value** is a signed byte, two's complement as the M8's transposes are: `01` to `1C` go up 1 to 28, `FF` to `E4` down 1 to 28, and `00` doesn't move it, so `FE` is two down. Of the issue's options, `x` for the way and `y` the amount stops at 15 and wastes `x`'s other values, and `01`–`7F` up with `81`–`FF` down reads `81` as one down, which no other value here does; two's complement is what a hex reader knows, and `=` and `-` go through `00` from one to the other. 28 is the Metropolix's furthest limit, so a value past it, `1D` to `E3`, is refused when typed and left out when read, as `CND` treats a value that isn't a condition. Choosing `ACU` with `K` goes on to a list of the amounts, `+1` to `+28` then `-1` to `-28`, with each one's value and what it does in degrees or semitones, and `K` on an `ACU`'s value lists them again, as a signed byte is awkward to type; `+` and `_` move it by 7, an octave of a seven-note scale. Two `ACU`s on a row add up. An `ACU` in a column with a lane is read from the lane's step and moves the note's row, as `DEL` is. **Degrees:** with the scale on, a note moves through the scale as `=` moves it (decision 24), a note outside it landing on the next one in; with it off, by semitones. The chain's transpose goes on after, so a transposed chain moves in the scale before it's shifted. This is the first thing that plays the song to read the scale, so decision 24's "nothing that plays the song reads it" now has this exception. As the scale is outside undo and isn't in the song the engine has, the app sends it to the engine as a live control, `SetScale`, as mutes are, when it changes and when a song loads, and a render takes the song's. **When it moves:** a row's first play since its accumulators started again is as written, and each later play adds its amount before its note sounds, which gives the manual's examples in both modes, as `Track` needs pass 1 played as written and pass 2 moved for every row. A row adds when its note plays: a row that `CHA` or `CND` skips adds nothing, as the Metropolix's `PROB` counts as a rest, and nor does a `RST`; a `HLD` adds once a row. **The Acc row:** the settings are the track's, `Track.accumulation`, set on the Mod page in a row of their own under the LFOs, `Acc`, as they move notes from pass to pass as LFOs move settings over time, and the Sound page's columns are full. They aren't registry settings, as an LFO's shape isn't: they shape the whole walk, and a lock lasting one note would change its range halfway. Its columns are `Up` and `Down`, the limits, 1 to 28 and 7 unless changed, an octave of a seven-note scale; `Moves`, `Row` or `Track`; `Limit`, `Wrap`, `Bounce` or `Hold`; `Adds`, `Row`, `Pulse` or `Retrig`; and `Reset`, `Chain`, `Play` or 1 to 16 loops. The status line names each in full, `Accumulator up` and so on, with what it does as set. `=` and `-` move each, the limits stopping at 1 and 28 and the resets at either end of their list while the short lists go round; `+` and `_` move a limit by 7; `i` types a limit; `K` lists the others with what each does; `x` sets one back. A return and the master, which have no notes, have no `Acc` row. **Row and Track mode,** as the Metropolix's `Stage` and `Track`: in `Row` each row with an `ACU` has its own accumulator and only its note moves; in `Track` the track has one, every row with an `ACU` adds to it, and every row on the track moves by it, rows without an `ACU` included, as the manual's example has, and across every phrase of the chain. A row is a phrase's row, so a phrase played twice in its chain moves on each time it's played, as a condition's pass counts it. **At a limit,** as the Metropolix's unipolar orders, which the issue's "Wrap (back to the start)" describes: `Wrap` carries on from the note as written, going round modulo the limit, so with `Up` at 3, `+1` plays 0 1 2 3 0 1 and `+3` with `Up` at 7 plays 0 3 6 1; `Bounce` turns back at the limit and again at the note as written, the Metropolix's pendulum, reflecting any step that goes past, so 0 1 2 3 2 1 0 1; `Hold` stays at the limit until it starts again. A positive amount goes between the note and `Up`, a negative one between `Down` and the note. In `Track` mode with amounts of both signs, the walk can be on either side, and only crossing a limit in the way it's going wraps, turns or holds it. The Metropolix's bipolar orders, which wrap to the other limit or swing between both, and its `Random` drunken walk, are left out; either could come as more choices of `Limit`, the walk on a random stream of the track's own as the groove's humanise is, so `CHA` rolls as it did. **When it adds,** `Adds`, for the whole track rather than per row as the Metropolix has it, as an `ACU`'s byte holds only its amount: `Row` once a row play, as the default; `Pulse` on every pulse of a `MLT` too, so `MLT4` with `+1` plays `C D E F`, then `G A B C`; `Retrig` on every hit of a `RET` too, the row's own note being the first hit. A `SGL` or `HLD` adds once a row whatever it's set to, as the Metropolix's table has a `HOLD` add once a stage play, and its single gate add on its first pulse alone. **When it starts again,** `Reset`, as an epoch the accumulators count in, and a new epoch puts every one of the track's back to the notes as written: `Chain`, the default, wherever the track's chain starts, so wherever a condition's passes start (decision 84): the top, a section that brings the chain in or resets, a launch, `:reset` and the song wrapping round, the Metropolix's `Auto`; `Play`, only when playing starts, so a line carries on across sections and chains, its `Manual`; and every 1 to 16 times round the chain, counted from where it started, by the count over the chain's length, as a condition's passes are. The issue's "every N passes" is taken as loops of the chain rather than of the row, as every row on the track then starts again together, which `Track` mode needs; with the phrase once in its chain, they're the same. Playing always starts them from the notes as written, from the top or from a step. **Kept, not worked out:** a condition's pass is worked out from the count, so playing from a step hears what playing up to it would, but what an accumulator has added depends on what played, `CHA`'s rolls among it, so it's kept as the track plays, and playing from a step starts it as written, as the Metropolix's reset does when it's started. Each track keeps a fixed table of 64 rows' accumulators, made ahead of time, with whether each row has played since they started again, its offset and whether a bounce has turned it, and the track's own for `Track` mode, so the audio thread never allocates; a row past 64 with an `ACU` plays as written, though in `Track` mode the track's still moves it. Each track counts how many times its chain has started, and each step keeps the count it was worked out after, so a step that lands after the next section has started still counts in its own. Nothing random is used, so renders repeat, and a song without an `ACU` plays exactly as before: both golden renders are unchanged. **Shown:** the grid shows notes as written, as the Metropolix's note names leave the accumulator out. While playing, the row under the playhead shows the note heard in its note column, before the chain's transpose, from a field on each track's place in the telemetry, `accumulated`, which is set only when an accumulator moved the note; and the status line on the Note column says `the row playing is heard as D-4`. On an `ACU`, it says `up 2 scale degrees each time it plays, wrap on this row`. **Values, a fourth mark, left for later** as the issue said, to decide once notes are in use: it would be `a` beside `.` `h` and `g` (decision on marks, M4), a `Mark::Accumulate` that `~` cycles to, so `CUT 08a` adds `08` to the track's held `CUT` each time its row plays. It would take its limits from the setting's own range rather than `Up` and `Down`, use the `Acc` row's `Limit`, `Adds` and `Reset`, and keep its walk in the same table, keyed by the row and its slot, with `Track` mode moving the held value under every row. It touches every place a mark is read, the step text's glyph and a list's or an event's having none, so it's a larger change than notes. **Alternatives:** `ACC`, taken by Accent; the issue's other two encodings, above; the settings as registry settings that steps lock, rejected above; a `Polarity` setting, which bipolar choices of `Limit` would cover more simply; `Adds` per row in the value's high digit, which would leave 15 as the largest amount; the limits as one range from `Down` to `Up` that wrap goes round, which made a rising line jump below the note at its limit and a falling one with `Down` at 0 jump to the top; and working the walk out from the count, which can't know what `CHA` skipped. Added on 2026-11-09.
86. **A row's `ARP xy` goes round its note, the note `x` up and the note `y` up, in scale degrees with the scale on and semitones with it off, for as long as the note lasts, and the `Arp` row on the Mod page says how fast, in what order, over how many octaves and whether each note starts again** (#232, the third of #229's sequencer logic). **Prior art:** Renoise's `-Axy` goes round the note, `x` and `y` semitones above it on every tick, 12 to a line unless changed, with `0` for `x` or `y` playing the note itself in its place, and changes only the pitch. The M8's `ARP XY` plays the note and `X` and `Y` semitones above it, so `ARP37` on C-4 is C-4, D#4 and G-4, and `ARC XY` sets its mode `X` and its speed `Y` in ticks, six to a step by default (manual v20260421, p. 69); its tables do the same with note offsets such as 00, 03, 07 and 0C run at tick speed, looped with `HOP`. Elektron's arpeggiator is a track's, with a mode, a speed in steps, a range in octaves and a length, each of its notes a trig of its own. **The command:** `ARP`, an event, picked with `K`, as the issue proposed; the label was free, as no setting's label is `ARP`. Its value is `xy`, two hex digits that each mean a note, as `RET`'s mean a fade and a rate, so it's typed as two digits after it's picked rather than chosen from a list, which `CND` and `ACU` need as their values are hard to type; `=` and `-` move `y` and `+` and `_` move `x`, each from 0 to F without carrying into the other, as `RET`'s do. `x` and `y` are scale degrees with the scale on, as the home row's notes are, so `ARP 24` is a triad in any scale, a note outside the scale moving onto the next one in as an `ACU`'s does, and semitones with it off, through the `SetScale` live control decision 85 added. A `0` leaves that one out, so `ARP 70` and `ARP 07` both go between the note and its fifth, the issue's two-note arpeggio, rather than Renoise's playing the note twice; `ARP 00` has only the note, which with more than one octave goes up its octaves and with one is no arpeggio at all. Up's order is as written, the note, `x` then `y`, not sorted, as Renoise's and the M8's are. Two on a row: the last column's wins, as they run left to right. An `ARP` in a column with a lane is read from the lane's step and plays the note's row, as `DEL` and `ACU` are. **The Arp row:** the settings are the track's, `Track.arpeggio`, on the Mod page in a row of their own under `Acc`, as both play a row's note on from what's written and the Sound page's columns are full. As with `Acc`, they aren't registry settings that a step locks: the M8 sets them per row with `ARC`, and the issue's comment noted that settings steps lock would come to the same thing, but that would add four settings to every track's `K` list and its LFOs' and envelopes' `Dest` for something set once a track, so it's left for later, when a row needs its own rate or mode. Its columns are `Rate`, `Tick`, a sixth of a step as `DEL` counts, or `1/128`, `1/64`, `1/32T`, `1/32`, `1/16T`, `1/16`, `1/8T` or `1/8`, `Tick` unless changed, the tracker feel the issue asked for, and never Elektron's speed and multiplier; `Mode`, `Up`, `Down`, `Up-down` or `Random`; `Oct`, 1 to 4, how many octaves it climbs before it starts again, 1 unless changed, as the issue proposed, four octaves of three notes being as many as a fast arpeggio can make out; and `Retrig`, `off` unless changed. The status line names each in full, `Arp rate` and so on, with what it does as set, and on an `ARP` it says `the note, 4 and 7 semitones up, Up at Tick, 1 octave, legato`. `=` and `-` move each, the rates and octaves stopping at either end and the modes going round; `i` types the octaves; `K` lists the others with what each does; `x` sets one back. A return and the master, which have no notes, have no `Arp` row. **Rate and Speed:** everything that's a share of a step is a share of the track's (decision 82), so a division is as long against the track's step as it is on a track at `1/16`, and on a track at `1/8` `Tick` is a twelfth of a beat. The divisions are named as on a track at `1/16`, the song's own step, with what each plays to a step, `two notes to a step` for `1/32`, in the list and on the status line, so they read right at any speed. Each note is counted from where the first started, by the frames in that many shares of the step, so a long note's don't drift. **The modes:** `Up` plays the note, `x` and `y`, then the same an octave up for each octave past the first; `Down` plays that backwards from the top, so a new note starts on its highest; `Up-down` goes up and back without playing the top and bottom twice; and `Random` picks any of them each time, from a stream of the track's own seeded from the project's seed, after every track's humanise stream (decision 80), so `CHA`'s rolls are as they would be without it, renders repeat and the golden renders are unchanged. Its first note is rolled too. A synth that draws noise for each note, struck again by an arpeggio with `Retrig` on, takes the noise's seed from the same stream, for the same reason. **Retrig:** off, each note only changes the pitch, as a tie does: a sample keeps playing at the new speed, a synth carries on without its envelopes starting again, and on `Acid`, as the issue asked, it slides, as a `HLD` does. That's the default, as Renoise's `-Axy` and the M8's `ARP` only move the pitch, and a voice's envelopes started again six times a step chop a sound up. On, each note starts again with the track's envelopes, as a `RET`'s repeat does, and on `Acid` that's a new 303 note that carries its filter and accent on, without a slide. **How long it lasts:** as long as its note, as a lock lasts its note (decision on marks, M4): through the row's pulses, `SGL`'s included, and the empty rows after while the note sounds, until the next note, an `OFF`, a `KIL`, a rest, or the note's gate closing (decisions 78 and 79), which lets the note's release ring on at the pitch it reached. The issue's "with `HLD` it carries into the next row" is taken as the tie: a `HLD` carries the arpeggio sounding into the note it ties into, which is still the same note (decision 79), so it goes on round from that note where it was, at the same rate and time, with that row's own `ARP` if it has one and otherwise the one it carried. Any other note, a new note in the issue's words, starts again with its own `ARP`, from the first of its notes, or has none. Ending it at the end of its row, as a `RET` ends, was rejected, as a note at a full `Gate` rings on past its row and would fall back to the note as written halfway, where a lock lasts its note. A row that `CHA` or `CND` skips leaves the note before, and its arpeggio, sounding, and an `ARP` on a row without a note does nothing, as there's no note for it to start with; changing the arpeggio of a note already sounding from a later row is left for later. **How it meets the rest:** it starts where its note does, after the groove, the row's nudge (decision 81) and any `DEL`, so a delayed note starts on its arpeggio's first note and the one before carries on at its last until then. Each pulse of a `MLT` is a new note, so each starts it again, as a `MLT`'s pulses take the row's `RET` and `ACU` again. A `RET`'s repeats strike the note the arpeggio is on, and an arpeggio's note due with a repeat goes first, so the repeat strikes it. Its notes are worked out from the note as it's heard, after an `ACU` has moved it, so an accumulator moves the arpeggio's root as it moves the row's note, and the chain's transpose goes on after the degrees, as it does for an `ACU`. It plays one note at a time on the track's one voice, as the issue said, so it isn't a chord; on the `Chord` voice each of its notes is a chord. **Engine:** a track keeps the sounding note's arpeggio, `Arpeggiating` in `track_state.rs`, with its notes, at most 12, worked out once as the note starts, its place, how many have played, where the first started and when the next is due, among the track's events beside `RET`'s, in a fixed size so the audio thread never allocates; a song without an `ARP` never makes one and plays exactly as before, so both golden renders are unchanged. **Left out:** the issue's later items, an arpeggio from the `Chord` voice's chord tables and an arpeggio that accumulates; Elektron's other modes, its note length and its arpeggiator's own trig length, as the note's `Gate` ends the arpeggio and `Retrig` with the envelopes gives each note its shape; a rate or mode per row, the M8's `ARC`; and an `ARP` that changes a note already sounding. **Alternatives:** choosing the value from a list, as `CND` and `ACU` do, which two hex digits that each mean a note don't need; Renoise's `0` playing the note again in its place, which the issue replaced with a two-note arpeggio; `Retrig` on by default, as Elektron's arpeggiator makes each note a trig, rejected for the tracker's feel and the chopping above; and the settings as registry settings, above. Added on 2026-11-09.
87. **A phrase plays its rows in an order, forward unless `:order` gives it another: reverse, pendulum, ping-pong, odd-even, converge, diverge, pedal, shuffle or random, after the Metropolix's ORDER, and a pass of the phrase is one time round its order** (#265, the fourth of #229's sequencer logic). **Prior art:** the Metropolix's ORDER screen (manual v1.6.5, pp. 69–73) has a Play Order and a Direction for each track: `Linear`, `Ping Pong` (`1 2 … 8 8 … 1`), `Pendulum` (`1 2 … 8 7 … 2`), `Odd/Even`, `Jump by` 2 to 5, `Hopscotch`, three `Arp` shifts, `Converge`, `Diverge`, `Growing`, `Shrinking` and `Pedal Point`, and the generative `Brownian` (a drunken walk, half forward, a quarter staying, a quarter back), `Shuffle` (a random order that then repeats until a reset deals a new one), `Quasi Random` (never the same stage twice) and `Random` (a die roll each stage); a stage's pulses play together whatever the order. Elektron has no order per pattern, though the Digitakt II's tracks have a direction; the M8 has none, and `HOP` jumps. **What's taken:** one list, as `K` lists choose everything else, rather than an order and a direction: `Forward`, `Reverse`, `Pendulum` and `Ping-pong`, both of the manual's ways of going down and back, which settles the issue's question of whether the end rows repeat by offering each, as the Metropolix does; `Odd-even`, `Converge`, `Diverge` and `Pedal`, which visit every row out of turn and with an `ACU` make the Metropolix's generative lines; and `Shuffle` and `Random`. Each fixed order plays exactly the manual's sequence for eight rows, and goes the same way for any length. **Left out:** the jumps, which leave rows unplayed, so they're `CND`'s work; `Hopscotch`, the `Arp` shifts, `Growing` and `Shrinking`, whose passes are several times the phrase, are niche and can come later as more choices; the separate Direction, as `Reverse` is the one reversed order that's often wanted, and the reversed generative orders mean little; `Quasi Random`; and `Brownian`, whose walk depends on every step before it, so it can't be worked out from where the track is, which every other order and a condition's pass can. It could come as an order that's kept, as accumulators are. **Where it's set:** the order is the phrase's, `Phrase.order`, left out of a file when it's forward, as `Track.speed` is at `1/16`. `:order pendulum` sets it on the cursor's phrase, as `:len` sets its length, and `:order` alone opens a list of the orders, each with what it plays in a phrase of four, on the one it plays, as `:groove` does; it completes the words, and `Action::SetOrder` makes it one undo step. A word that isn't an order is refused with the list. The phrase view's title names an order other than forward after the phrase's slot, `Phrase 00 1/1 Pendulum`, as it names a lane's length. It isn't a `K` list on a cell, as the header has no cursor, nor a command, as the issue said, and a chain slot or a step can't override it, so a phrase shared between chains plays the same order in each, and `gc` copies it to give one place its own; a chain slot's order, as it has a transpose, is left for later if sharing a phrase in two orders is wanted. **What a pass walks:** the rows of the phrase's loop, so its live length is the order's length, and the order is worked out over them each time: a pass is a list of visits, each a row, and the note column walks them as it walked rows, adding each visit's pulses, so `MLT`, `SGL` and `HLD` pulses stay together, a row of 0 pulses takes no time, and `Phrase::time()` is the pass's length. `Pendulum` and `Pedal` visit 2n−2 rows and `Ping-pong` 2n, so their passes, their chains and a section whose length they set last longer, a pendulum of 16 rows being 30 steps, as the Metropolix's pendulum of 8 is 14; the rest keep a pass's length to its rows'. `Random` picks until the pass has lasted as long as the rows would forward, cutting its last pick short if it must, as the Metropolix's pulse limit cuts a stage, so a phrase's length never depends on its picks; it never picks a row of 0 pulses. What plays next is the next visit, so a `HLD` ties into whichever row the order plays next, across the end of a pass too, and a glide looks for its next value in the order the rows play, over the time between in that order; for forward that's what it was. **Random orders** are dealt by a hash, `Dealer` in `vt-model`'s `order.rs`, of the project's seed, the track, which time round its chain this is, counted from where the chain started, and the chain slot, so a phrase twice in a chain is dealt twice. As it's a hash and not the track's generator, it takes nothing from the streams `CHA`, the groove's humanise and a `Random` arpeggio roll from, and which rows a pass plays is worked out from where the track is, as a condition's pass is: a render repeats, playing from a step hears what playing up to it would, and the screen draws the playhead from the same hash. `Shuffle` deals every row once a pass, a new deal each pass, as a deck shuffled again, by a small Feistel network walked until it lands among the rows, which works one visit out at a time without a table. The issue asked whether it's a shuffle or a free pick, and both are kept, as the Metropolix keeps both, which it explains as cards against dice. A chain that starts again, at a section that brings it in, a launch, `:reset` or the song wrapping round, deals again from its first time round, so a song plays the same every time through, while a section that repeats its chain deals each pass anew. The Metropolix's shuffle, which repeats one deal until a reset, was left out, as it's the same as this one with the phrase once in a chain that starts every pass; it could be a choice of its own. **Lanes** keep their own forward loops, as they count steps, not rows, and they're the grid the notes move over; a lane's glide looks in its lane, as before. **Passes:** a condition's pass is a time round the order, worked out from the count as decision 84 has it, so it's unchanged for every order: a row that a pendulum visits twice is in the same pass both times, and a `1:2` plays both visits or neither. Counting each visit as a pass was rejected, as with `Random` the number of visits to a row isn't known without playing the pass, and `1:2` reading "every other time round" holds for every order. An accumulator adds each time its row plays (decision 85), so a row a pendulum plays twice moves on twice a pass. Its `Reset` every so many loops counts the chain's loops, which are longer in a pendulum. **Time:** a track's `Speed` sets how long each visit's pulses are, so a reverse phrase at `1/8` plays its rows backwards on eighths. The groove pushes each step by its place in the track's count, not by its row, so the swing stays on the same steps whatever plays there, and a row's nudge goes with its row, as it's the row's. **Live changes:** changing a phrase's order or its length while it plays keeps the track on the row it was about to play, as a live length does: at the same visit if the new pass plays that row there, which every edit that leaves the order alone does, and otherwise where the new order first plays the row, from where it carries on in the new order. **Shown:** the playhead is on the row playing, and the other tracks' columns beside the grid show theirs, both from the same lookups the engine makes with the same hash, so they jump with the order.
